[02:26:59.908–02:27:02.690] SPEAKER_15: such a moratorium would have no legal effect.

[02:27:03.371–02:27:08.655] SPEAKER_15: Everyone would be entitled to continue to rely on the existing zoning rules.

[02:27:10.537–02:27:16.702] SPEAKER_15: Now it's true that no one has the right to the continuation of existing zoning laws.

[02:27:17.583–02:27:27.872] SPEAKER_15: However, rights under existing zoning laws vest when a person reasonably believes they will be allowed to develop property under existing law and this

[02:27:28.443–02:27:34.645] SPEAKER_15: REASONABLE BELIEF RISES BECAUSE NO OFFICIAL ACTION HAS TAKEN PLACE TO CHANGE THE EXISTING LAW.

[02:27:35.225–02:27:38.606] SPEAKER_15: AND SECONDLY, THEY HAVE SUBSTANTIALLY CHANGED THEIR POSITION.

[02:27:39.707–02:27:44.448] SPEAKER_15: AN EXAMPLE OF THAT IS INCURRING SUBSTANTIAL EXPENSES BASED ON THIS REASONABLE BELIEF.

[02:27:45.689–02:27:56.212] SPEAKER_15: SO THE ONLY WAY THE COUNTY CAN PREVENT PEOPLE FROM ALIGNING ON EXISTING ZONING RULES IS TO FORMALLY START THE PROCESS OF CHANGING THE EXISTING LAW.

[02:27:57.182–02:28:02.866] SPEAKER_15: Amending the county zoning ordinance requires a text amendment pursuant to Illinois statute.

[02:28:03.446–02:28:15.373] SPEAKER_15: This involves introducing proposed amendments before the zoning committee, which if approved, they next go before the zoning board of appeals, and then finally they're considered by the county board.

[02:28:16.734–02:28:22.678] SPEAKER_15: So for purposes of ending reasonable reliance on an existing zoning rule,

[02:28:25.720–02:28:34.227] SPEAKER_15: the time would it be reasonable to rely on existing zoning rules would occur at the time the proposed amendment to that rule was presented to the committee.

[02:28:34.887–02:28:39.811] SPEAKER_15: So before the formal process starts to change a rule, people can rely on the old rule.

[02:28:40.992–02:28:53.943] SPEAKER_15: So in my opinion, while passing a resolution called a moratorium may send a signal to potential developers that the county is opposed to granting zoning approval for data storage centers under its existing zoning law,

[02:28:54.402–02:28:57.183] SPEAKER_15: SUCH A RESOLUTION HAS NO LEGAL EFFECT.

[02:28:58.124–02:29:13.572] SPEAKER_15: AGAIN, IF BOARD MEMBERS WANT TO ACHIEVE THE EFFECT OF A MORATORIUM, THEY SHOULD SPONSOR A RESOLUTION WITH THE BROAD CHANGES THEY DESIRE TO THE COUNTY CODE PROVISIONS PRESENTLY GOVERNING THE ZONING OF DATA STORAGE CENTERS.

[02:29:16.689–02:29:26.674] SPEAKER_15: My opinion relies on an Illinois Supreme Court opinion, 1350 Lakeshore Associates versus Healy, 223 Illinois 2nd, 607.
